HIS DIAGNOSIS. There is one plausible one-tier theorem within striking distance, but ONLY in
a fairly strong form. It is NOT "the same realization with local coefficients". It must turn
the cover-dependent cocycle construction into a REFINEMENT-COHERENT, PRESENTATION-INDEPENDENT
construction attached to the standard datum (X, A, (omega_lambda)), and it must identify
exactly the unavoidable H^1 and H^0 ambiguities. He calls this a hard but recognizable
extension of the present proof.

He is equally explicit about what is reachable but would NOT raise the tier, and you must not
spend effort on any of it: removing H^1(N, Z) = 0; adding Ext-labelled examples; allowing more
finite groups; replacing the ordinary nerve by a larger Cech complex.

TARGET - Theorem A: refinement-coherent marked realization and intrinsic homological image.
Let X be a connected finite polyhedron, A a finite locally constant sheaf of abelian groups on
X, Lambda a finite nonempty set, and omega_lambda in H^2(X, A) for lambda in Lambda, with
Lambda_0 = {lambda : omega_lambda = 0}. For a finite Leray cover U = {U_i} of X, NOT assumed
to have connected intersections, use
    C^q(U, A) = product over i_0 < ... < i_q of Gamma(U_{i_0...i_q}, A),
that is, the ACTUAL section groups on possibly disconnected overlaps, rather than one copy of
a constant group per ordinary-nerve simplex. For each presentation choose Cech cocycles
alpha_lambda in Z^2(U, A) with [alpha_lambda] = omega_lambda, and for each lambda in Lambda_0
a null-homotopy. Take the full statement, including the refinement-coherence requirement and
the exact H^1 and H^0 ambiguity identification, from HIS report rather than from this summary.

WHAT MAKES THIS THE TIER-RAISING VERSION, and therefore what you may not compromise: the
construction must be attached to the STANDARD DATUM (X, A, (omega_lambda)) and be independent
of the chosen presentation and coherent under refinement. The present manuscript is openly
presentation-relative, begins from supplied lift data, and does not claim path independence
across refinement zigzags - its conclusion says the appendix records only word-by-word
naturality. If the new construction remains presentation-relative, or if refinement coherence
is asserted only along chosen zigzags, the target has not been met.

WHAT ALREADY FEEDS THIS: the manuscript's two genuinely constructive parts, the
representative-rigidity theorem and the finite-label cocycle realization. Read them first and
say which parts carry over unchanged and which must be rebuilt for disconnected overlaps.

HONEST OUTCOMES: this is hard. If it does not close, say so in the FIRST LINE of your report
and give the exact step that fails - in particular whether the obstruction is in refinement
coherence, in presentation independence, or in pinning the H^1/H^0 ambiguities. A precise
obstruction is a real result. Do not deliver a presentation-relative construction relabelled
as intrinsic, and do not quietly assume connected intersections, which is exactly the
simplification this theorem is meant to remove.

Overall diagnosis
There is one plausible one-tier theorem within striking distance of the present article, but only in a fairly strong form. It is not â€œthe same realization with local coefficients.â€ It must turn the cover-dependent cocycle construction into a refinement-coherent, presentation-independent construction attached to the standard datum (X,A,(Ï‰Î»â€‹)), and it must identify exactly the unavoidable H1- and H0-ambiguities.
That is a hard but recognizable extension of the present proof. By contrast, removing H1â€‹(N,Z)=0, adding Ext-labelled examples, allowing more finite groups, or replacing the ordinary nerve by a larger ÄŒech complex would be reachable but would not raise the mathematical tier.
The manuscript itself correctly exposes the present ceiling: it begins with supplied lift data, is deliberately finite and presentation-relative, and does not claim path independence across refinement zigzags.   Its two genuinely constructive parts are the representative-rigidity theorem and the finite-label cocycle realization; the conclusion expressly says that the appendix records only word-by-word naturality, not path independence, and contains none of the source-side machinery needed for crossed-module comparison.

The article currently proves a single-cover construction under an ordinary-nerve coefficient identification. The actual construction already controls the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutrality set, and selected evaluation maps. That is the only available move which simultaneously addresses significance, architecture, and presentation dependence.
It must, however, be stronger than ordinary gerbe classification. The genuinely paper-specific part has to be the marked prestack-level package, including the terminal fibre, zero-component null-homotopies, refinement 2-coherence, and the exact H1/H0 comparison ambiguity.

2. Reachability from the machinery in main.pdf
Theorem A: reachable as a hard extension, but only in the finite-polyhedral form
Existing ingredients that genuinely feed the proof
The current paper already contains almost all of the object-level formulas needed.
The change-of-choice calculation shows that changing overlap arrows by a 1-cochain changes the triple defect by a coboundary, while changing local objects produces the same class up to a further 1-cochain. It also records literal naturality under a band map. 
The prescribed-realization proof constructs the twisted groupoid by
(c,j,k)âˆ˜(b,i,j)=(b+c+Î±Î»,ijkâ€‹,i,k),
and proves associativity exactly from the cocycle equation.   Its restriction functors are already strict, and the proof verifies that the chosen ÄŒech cocycle is recovered exactly as the component gerbeâ€™s triple defect.  
Proposition 4.1 already proves compatibility of the cochain identification with refinement maps and establishes cofinality of subdivision covers.   UCT naturality under simplicial maps and coefficient maps is written out explicitly. 
The paper also identifies precisely what fails on disconnected overlaps: the section group is AÏ€0â€‹(UJâ€‹), so an ordinary nerve carrying one copy of A does not retain enough data.  In the realization proof it again notes that the defect would be a tuple on a disconnected triple overlap and would not be specified by an ordinary-nerve cocycle.  This makes the replacement machinery clear: use the actual ÄŒech section complex rather than the ordinary simplicial cochain complex.
Genuinely new missing ingredient
The missing work is not another cocycle calculation. It is the construction and verification of the whole comparison 2-category:


explicit functors induced by 1-cochain gauges;


explicit natural transformations induced by 0-cochains;


compatibility of those operations with the terminal neutralizations bÎ»â€‹;


comparison equivalences for refinements;


unit, associativity, and interchange coherence;


independence of arbitrary refinement zigzags;


identification of the exact H1-torsor and H0-automorphism ambiguities;


replacement of ordinary-nerve evaluation by the intrinsic cap-product map into H0â€‹(X,A).


There is a delicate obstruction hidden in the zero components. A zero cohomology class does not possess a functorially chosen zero cocycle. To put a terminal object into that component of the prestack, one must carry a chosen null-homotopy Î´bÎ»â€‹=Î±Î»â€‹, and those choices themselves have H1-ambiguity. A correct theorem must incorporate that ambiguity rather than conceal it with a nonfunctorial choice of Î±Î»â€‹=0.
Restricted to finite polyhedra, finite Leray covers, and finite local systems, I regard this as a hard extension of the present proof, not a separate research programme. Extending it instead to arbitrary sites, arbitrary hypercovers, and a fully homotopy-coherent functor on spaces would be a new higher-stack project. General hypercover classification is already standard, so such breadth by itself would not constitute the novelty. arXiv+1
Theorem B: reachable, but with a substantial standardness risk
The paper has already located the cocycle: it is the ratio between the two composites of pullback comparison arrows.  The local pullback of that defect to a matching family is exactly the same type of triple-overlap defect used to compute a component gerbe class.
The missing ingredients are:


the induced topology and coefficient sheaf on âˆ«Câ€‹F;


a proof that the coherence defects define a global choice-independent class there;


the precise strictification statementâ€”vanishing gives a cartesian pseudosection or an equivalent strict model, not necessarily literal equalities inside the original fixed cleavage;


the pullback theorem identifying the relative class with every component gerbe class.


This is achievable with relative gerbe theory or cohomology of fibred categories. The danger is that a referee may identify Îº(P/F) simply as the classical class of the gerbe over the component object. Moerdijkâ€™s cocycle classification already shows how local object choices and comparison arrows produce the degree-two class of a banded gerbe. arXiv+1 Unless the strictification and terminal consequences go materially beyond that standard result, Theorem B will improve the exposition more than the tier.
Theorem C: directly reachable
The proof of Theorem 4.2 uses H1â€‹(N,Z)=0 only to turn the UCT map into an isomorphism and to infer
Ï•Î»â€‹=0âŸºÏ‰Î»â€‹=0.
After actual classes Ï‰Î»â€‹ are supplied, one simply chooses normalized cocycles representing them and repeats formula (15). The groupoid construction itself does not use H1â€‹=0. 
This should be provable correctly with very little new machinery. It does not, however, overcome the supplied-data or presentation-relative architecture.
Theorem D: not reachable
The present article starts after a gerbe class and a ÄŒech representative have been supplied. It has no locally smooth group cochain complex, no crossed-module characteristic cocycle, no construction of the map Ï„, no comparison cochain, and no proof of choice independence or naturality in the Lie-group setting. The manuscript says this explicitly. 
NWWâ€™s problem compares two classes generated from the same source-side geometric extension. The current realization theorem instead begins with the target H2-class and constructs a prestack realizing it. Reversing that direction would require substantially different machinery, not an elaboration of formula (15). Problem 8.1(b) itself spells out the locally continuous 3-cocycle and the expected equality with the lifting-gerbe class. arXiv

Main failure modes
Theorem A
The proof may succeed at the level of stackification but fail to preserve the marked prestack dataâ€”especially the chosen terminal neutralizationsâ€”coherently under refinements. A second failure mode is priority: if the final result reduces to the usual equivalence between the 2-groupoid of A-gerbes and the degree-two cohomology 2-type, then the additional labels may again be judged bookkeeping. Moerdijkâ€™s theorem already gives the cover-colimit classification at the level of gerbes. arXiv
The theorem earns its impact score only if the proof genuinely controls the marked prestack, terminal fibre, and exact comparison ambiguity across the presentation category.
Theorem B
The class Îº(P/F) may turn out to be no more than the standard relative Giraud class. Also, vanishing may yield a coherent pseudosection only after replacing P by an equivalent fibred category; it need not produce literal pullback equalities in the original split cleavage. An overstrong strictification claim would be false.
Theorem C
The principal failure is not correctness but significance. Even a perfect proof would be read as: â€œreplace the UCT isomorphism by a UCT fibre and prescribe the class rather than its evaluation.â€ The paper already states the relevant Ext phenomenon and its effect on the homological image. 
Theorem D
The project would have to develop an independent locally smooth degree-three theory, not merely use the current gerbe cocycles. The chance estimate reflects mismatch of machinery, not the mathematical importance of the problem.

4. Which tier-raising levers genuinely apply?
(a) Settling a named open problem: does not apply
The only identified named problem with appropriate potential impact is NWW Problem 8.1(b). It asks for the explicit equality between the transgressed crossed-module characteristic H3-class and the lifting-gerbe ÄŒech H2-class. arXiv
That problem is not reachable from the articleâ€™s current direction of construction. The article realizes a supplied H2-class; NWW requires deriving and comparing two classes from crossed-module and principal-bundle data. The absence of source-side degree-three machinery is structural, not a missing lemma. 
No weaker â€œpartial NWWâ€ statement obtained by testing examples or by starting from an already identified H2-class would count as settling the problem.
(b) Proving something about standard objects: applies, through Theorem A
The standard object should be
(X,A,(Ï‰Î»â€‹)),
where X is a finite polyhedron, A is a local system, and the Ï‰Î»â€‹ are ordinary sheaf-cohomology classes. The theoremâ€™s intrinsic invariant is
KÎ»intrâ€‹=Im(H2â€‹(X,Z)Ï‰Î»â€‹âŒ¢âˆ’â€‹H0â€‹(X,A)),
not an image attached to a chosen nerve.
The comparison standard is the classical classification of banded gerbes by H2, including construction from cocycles. arXiv The new content must be a coherent theorem about the articleâ€™s marked prestack presentations, not merely another realization of the standard gerbe.
This is the strongest applicable lever because it directly removes the current dependence on author-selected cover and coefficient trivializations.
A theorem about bare empirical models would also use standard objects, but the current paper establishes a negative boundary rather than a source construction: the canonical split lift has neutral component gerbes, and non-split lift data are not selected by the empirical model.   Creating a canonical non-split lift from empirical data would therefore be a new semantic construction, not a reachable extension of the current finite-site realization.
(c) Removing a hypothesis: applies only in a structural combined form
Removing H1â€‹(N,Z)=0
This yields Theorem C. It is correct and useful, but it does not raise the tier.
The hypothesis currently guarantees that evaluation detects the full class. Once it is removed, nonzero Ext classes can have zero homological image, exactly as the manuscript records.  Thus the existing quotient invariant becomes less complete, not more structural. Merely classifying which UCT fibre was chosen is standard homological algebra.
Removing connected-overlap and coherent constant-coefficient assumptions
Removing these hypotheses alone is technical. The manuscript correctly proves that the ordinary nerve cannot encode the section groups on disconnected overlaps.  The correct replacement is the full ÄŒech complex
âˆÎ“(Ui0â€‹â‹¯iqâ€‹â€‹,A),
or a component ÄŒech nerve/local-coefficient model.
This contributes to a tier-raising theorem only when combined with:


refinement 2-coherence;


presentation independence;


intrinsic cap-product images;


exact comparison ambiguity.


That combined theorem is Theorem A.
Removing finite-site restrictions
Passing from a finite cover to general hypercovers is not by itself a tier-raising result. Hyper-ÄŒech and homotopy classifications of sheaf cohomology and higher bundles already exist in much greater generality. arXiv+1
A broader site theorem would matter only if it proved something new about the marked terminal-prestack structure or yielded a nonformal standard-object application. Otherwise it would increase technical generality while decreasing the visibility of the only paper-specific construction.
Removing representative rigidity
Theorem B is the correct form of this lever. Literal pullback equality should be replaced by an invariant coherence-defect class and a sharp strictification criterion.
This could materially improve the paper because representative-rigidity is currently a presentation-level condition. But the lift remains supplied, and the obstruction is close to classical relative gerbe theory. I expect a moderate mathematical improvement rather than a full one-tier jump unless the theorem gives a genuinely new classification of marked prestack splittings.
Removing presentation dependence
This is the decisive hypothesis removal. It changes the status of KÏ‰â€‹, Qlabâ€‹, and Qcomâ€‹ from chosen-presentation outputs into invariants of (X,A,Ï‰). It also addresses the architecture criticism directly: the finite cover becomes a computational model rather than part of the mathematical datum.
The precise target is Theorem A, not a bare assertion that â€œUCT is natural under refinements.â€ The latter is already used in the manuscript and is standard.
(d) Sharpness or a matching bound: does not apply as the main lever
The current wedge theorem already has a matching necessary-and-sufficient bound,
d(G)â‰¤2Î²
together with the exclusion of cyclic prime-power groups. The paper correctly presents this as the sharp arithmetic endpoint of a selected realization, not as a new structural classification.  Its proof reduces to generator counts and decomposition of finite abelian groups. 
Generalizing this to m components, optimizing numbers of labels, or adding more subgroup-lattice criteria would remain elementary arithmetic appended to the same supplied realization. It would not address the paperâ€™s fundamental ceiling.
The H1/H0 ambiguity clause in Theorem A is a valuable optimality statement, but it supports the functorial theorem rather than constituting an independent tier-raising bound.

5. Strongest remaining objection after Theorem A
The hardest higher-tier referee objection would be:

â€œYou have produced an explicit coherent presentation of the standard degree-two classification of abelian-banded gerbes. The local-system cap image is an intrinsic reformulation of UCT evaluation, and the H1/H0 ambiguity is the standard automorphism theory of gerbes. The component labels and terminal prestack fibre remain bookkeeping imposed on prescribed classes. What new theorem has been obtained about a naturally occurring gerbe, moduli problem, or obstruction?â€

This objection would remain serious even if every coherence diagram in Theorem A were correct. Classical gerbe theory already classifies banded gerbes by degree-two cocycles, including the passage from cocycles to sheaves of groupoids. arXiv+1 General homotopy-theoretic frameworks already make higher bundle classification presentation-independent. arXiv
What would resolve that objection
The cleanest resolution would be a second theorem showing that the marked realization is not merely one construction but a complete moduli theorem.
Required resolution theorem â€” Biequivalence classification of marked finite presentations
For fixed (X,A,Î›), let
MarkRealXâ€‹(A,Î›)
be the bicategory whose objects are all locally split finite-Leray presentations of A-banded prestack lifts having:


marked component sheaf Î›â€‹;


a specified set Î›0â€‹ of terminally represented components;


terminal essential surjectivity;


locally connected component fibres;


and whose 1- and 2-morphisms are refinement-local marked banded equivalences and their natural isomorphisms.
Let
ZË‡aug2â€‹(X,A;Î›)
be the homotopy colimit over finite Leray covers of the augmented ÄŒech cocycle 2-groupoids, including the chosen null-homotopies in the zero components.
Prove that the realization 2-functor of Theorem A is a biequivalence
ZË‡aug2â€‹(X,A;Î›)â‰ƒMarkRealXâ€‹(A,Î›).
In particular:


every marked locally split prestack presentation is refinement-locally equivalent to one obtained from the twisted composition formula;


every marked banded equivalence is represented by a 1-cochain gauge;


every natural isomorphism is represented by a 0-cochain;


the only comparison ambiguities are the predicted H1 and H0 groups.


That theorem would convert the current â€œone explicit packagingâ€ into a classification of a natural bicategory of presentations. It would directly answer the objection that the construction is merely a chosen implementation.
Even then, an external application to a naturally occurring familyâ€”such as lifting gerbes, equivariant gerbes, or another standard source of nonconstant bandsâ€”would remain highly desirable. But the biequivalence would be enough to establish that the marked terminal-prestack structure itself has a genuine moduli theory rather than being arbitrary scaffolding.
Final recommendation on the mathematical route
The stop/go criterion should be strict:


Proceed only with Theorem A at full refinement-coherent strength, preferably followed by the marked-presentation biequivalence.


Do not treat Theorem C, more finite-group classifications, more quotient identities, additional wedge examples, or an isolated local-coefficient rewrite as tier-raising.


Do not pursue NWW within this paper.


Theorem B is a worthwhile secondary project, but it is less likely than Theorem A to overcome the supplied-lift and classical-antecedent objection.


If the refinement comparisons cannot be made into a coherent 2-functor preserving the marked terminal fibre, then the honest conclusion is that no result reachable from the present machinery raises the paper by a genuine tier. The remaining accessible extensions would all elaborate the same classical H2-realization architecture rather than change its mathematical status.

\section{Presentation-comparison bookkeeping}
\label{sec:presentation-bookkeeping}

The article deliberately keeps the homological image attached to supplied
finite-site data.  This section records the limited naturality that is useful
when two such presentations have been related explicitly.  It does not assert
path independence for arbitrary zigzags and does not turn the homological
image into an invariant of an unspecified geometric object.

Fix a finite abelian group \(A\).  A \emph{finite presentation} is a pair

\[
 (N,\omega),
 \qquad N\text{ a finite simplicial complex},\quad
 \omega\in H^2(N,A).
\]

Its evaluation homomorphism and image are

\[
 e_\omega:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A,
 \qquad K_\omega=\operatorname{Im}(e_\omega).
\]

The notation suppresses the universal coefficient transformation.  It is
important that the source group remains visible: an equality between image
subgroups can be justified by naturality only after the relevant map on
homology and the relevant map on coefficients have both been specified.

We use three elementary comparison moves.

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item A \emph{retri-vialization move} replaces a normalized cocycle
      \(\alpha\) by \(\alpha+\delta b\) on the same nerve.  It fixes the
      class \(\omega=[\alpha]\), hence fixes \(e_\omega\) and
      \(K_\omega\).
\item A \emph{simplicial move} is a simplicial map \(f:N'\to N\), carrying
      \((N,\omega)\) to \((N',f^*\omega)\).
\item A \emph{coefficient move} is an isomorphism
      \(\theta:A\xrightarrow{\sim}A'\), carrying \((N,\omega)\) to
      \((N,\theta_*\omega)\).
\end{enumerate}

Let \(\mathscr P_A\) denote the free groupoid generated by chosen invertible
instances of these moves.  Thus a morphism in \(\mathscr P_A\) remembers its
word in the selected comparison data.  Relations are imposed only for
identity moves, inverses, and literal compositions of the underlying maps.
In particular, two different refinement zigzags with the same endpoints are
not declared equal merely because they compare the same pair of nerves.

\begin{proposition}[Naturality along a specified presentation comparison]
\label{prop:presentation-comparison-naturality}
Let a morphism in the presentation groupoid be represented by a simplicial
isomorphism \(f:N'\to N\), a coefficient isomorphism
\(\theta:A\to A'\), and any number of retrivialization moves, so that

\[
 \omega'=\theta_*f^*\omega\in H^2(N',A').
\]

Then

\begin{equation}\label{eq:presentation-evaluation-square}
\begin{CD}
H_2(N',\mathbb Z) @>{e_{\omega'}}>> A'\\
@V{f_*}V{\cong}V @A{\theta}A{\cong}A\\
H_2(N,\mathbb Z) @>{e_\omega}>> A
\end{CD}
\end{equation}

commutes.  Consequently

\[
 K_{\omega'}=\theta(K_\omega),
 \qquad
 A'/K_{\omega'}\cong A/K_\omega.
\]

These identifications are functorial in the chosen word of comparison moves.
No equality between the identifications attached to distinct words is
asserted without an additional coherence argument.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The universal coefficient map is natural in the simplicial complex and in
the coefficient group.  If \(z\in H_2(N',\mathbb Z)\), then

\[
 e_{\omega'}(z)
 =e_{\theta_*f^*\omega}(z)
 =\theta\bigl(e_\omega(f_*z)\bigr),
\]

which is the commutativity of
\eqref{eq:presentation-evaluation-square}.  Since \(f_*\) and \(\theta\)
are isomorphisms, taking images gives
\(K_{\omega'}=\theta(K_\omega)\).  The induced quotient isomorphism is
\(a+K_\omega\mapsto\theta(a)+K_{\omega'}\).

A retrivialization changes a cocycle by a coboundary and hence does not
change its cohomology class.  The calculation therefore applies to each
generator.  Naturality under a composite follows by pasting the corresponding
squares.  Because the groupoid is free on the selected moves, this proves
functoriality along the recorded word and nothing stronger.
\end{proof}

The same calculation applies componentwise to the family in
\Cref{art-thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}.  Sums and
intersections are carried through a coefficient isomorphism, so the two
aggregate quotients in \Cref{art-prop:uct-aggregate-package} are transported
as well.  This is the entire presentation-comparison claim used by the
article.

\subsection{Cocycle changes in coordinates}

For completeness, choose a total order on the vertex set of \(N\).  A
normalized \(2\)-cochain is a function

\[
 \alpha_{ijk}\in A
 \qquad(i<j<k,\ \{i,j,k\}\in N),
\]

extended alternately to other orders and by zero to repeated indices.  Its
cocycle condition on an ordered \(3\)-simplex is

\begin{equation}\label{eq:appendix-cocycle-identity}
 \alpha_{jkl}-\alpha_{ikl}+\alpha_{ijl}-\alpha_{ijk}=0.
\end{equation}

For a \(1\)-cochain \(b=(b_{ij})\), retrivialization gives

\begin{equation}\label{eq:appendix-coboundary-change}
 \alpha'_{ijk}
 =\alpha_{ijk}+(\delta b)_{ijk}
 =\alpha_{ijk}+b_{jk}-b_{ik}+b_{ij}.
\end{equation}

If \(z=\sum n_{ijk}[i,j,k]\) is a simplicial \(2\)-cycle, then

\[
 \langle\delta b,z\rangle
 =\langle b,\partial z\rangle=0.
\]

Thus the coordinate calculation already shows that evaluation on homology
is unaffected by the choice of overlap arrows.  Changing local objects adds
another coboundary after conjugating the overlap arrows.  For an abelian
band, conjugation acts trivially on the coefficient sheaf, and the same
calculation applies.  This is the coordinate form of the standard
naturality statement used in the article's component-gerbe discussion.

The subgroup generated by the individual coordinates

\[
 H_\alpha=\langle\alpha_{ijk}\rangle\le A
\]

need not be unchanged by \eqref{eq:appendix-coboundary-change}.  It follows
that the coordinate quotient \(A/H_\alpha\) is not a substitute for the
homological quotient \(A/K_\omega\).  One always has

\[
 K_\omega\subseteq H_\alpha,
\]

because evaluating \(\alpha\) on a cycle forms an integral linear
combination of its coordinates, but strict containment can occur.  This is
why the article formulates its quotient through the UCT evaluation map.

\section{Finite calculations}
\label{sec:finite-calculations}

The following computations illustrate the scope and the limits of the
single UCT package in the article.  They are examples, not additional
classification results.

\subsection{A cyclic coefficient group}

Let \(A=\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z\), let
\(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong\mathbb Z^2\), and suppose
\(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\).
The homomorphism

\[
 \phi(m,n)=2m\pmod 4
\]

has image \(K=\{0,2\}\).  Hence

\[
 Q=A/K\cong\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z.
\]

Writing characters as

\[
 \chi_t(x)=\frac{tx}{4}\pmod{\mathbb Z},
 \qquad t\in\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z,
\]

the annihilator of \(K\) is

\[
 K^\perp=\{\chi_0,\chi_2\}.
\]

These are exactly the characters that factor through \(Q\).  The other two
characters distinguish elements within a quotient fibre and therefore do
not descend.

Now take two component images

\[
 K_1=\{0,2\},\qquad K_2=A.
\]

Then

\[
 \bigcap_iK_i=K_1,
 \qquad
 \sum_iK_i=A,
\]

and the aggregate exact sequence becomes

\[
 0\longrightarrow A/K_1
 \longrightarrow A/K_1
 \longrightarrow 0\longrightarrow0.
\]

This degenerate endpoint is useful as a check on directions: the labelled
quotient retains the first component's parity, while the common quotient
retains nothing.

\subsection{An Ext-only class}

Let \(N\) have the homology of \(\mathbb RP^2\), and let
\(A=\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z\).  Thus

\[
 H_2(N,\mathbb Z)=0,
 \qquad
 H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z.
\]

The universal coefficient sequence yields

\[
 H^2(N,A)
 \cong
 \operatorname{Ext}^1(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z,\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)
 \cong\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z.
\]

For the nonzero class \(\omega\), the evaluation map has zero domain and
therefore \(K_\omega=0\).  Its homological quotient is all of \(A\), even
though the corresponding banded gerbe is non-neutral.  This example keeps
separate two questions that can otherwise be conflated:

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
\item whether the Giraud class vanishes;
\item whether evaluation on integral \(2\)-cycles detects it.
\end{enumerate}

The first answer is no and the second answer is also no.  The homological
image records only the second question.

\subsection{Two complementary images}

Let \(A=(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^2\) with basis \(e_1,e_2\), and take

\[
 K_1=\langle e_1\rangle,
 \qquad
 K_2=\langle e_2\rangle.
\]

Then

\[
 K_1\cap K_2=0,
 \qquad
 K_1+K_2=A.
\]

Thus \(Q_{\mathrm{lab}}=A\), \(Q_{\mathrm{com}}=0\), and the kernel of the
aggregate comparison is all of \(A\).  Both component images are nonzero
and proper.  A map from \(\mathbb Z^\beta\) can realize either image as soon
as \(\beta\ge1\), and the two maps can be placed in separate components by
the construction of \Cref{art-thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}.

For a cyclic group of prime-power order, any two nonzero subgroups are
linearly ordered.  Their intersection is therefore nonzero, so this
complementary pattern cannot occur.  For a cyclic group whose order has two
distinct prime divisors, the primary subgroups for the two primes have zero
intersection and generate the whole group.  These elementary cases account
for the exceptional clause in
\Cref{art-thm:wedge-sphere-two-component-label-loss}.

\subsection{A matrix check}

When \(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong\mathbb Z^\beta\) and

\[
 A\cong\bigoplus_{r=1}^s\mathbb Z/n_r\mathbb Z,
\]

a homomorphism \(\phi:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A\) is represented by an
\(s\times\beta\) matrix whose \(r\)-th row is read modulo \(n_r\).  The
subgroup \(K_\omega\) is the column span in \(A\).  This gives a direct
finite check of every quotient in the article:

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item compute the column span for each component matrix;
\item compute intersections and sums inside the invariant-factor model of
      \(A\);
\item quotient \(A\) by the resulting subgroup;
\item check the character description by solving the corresponding modular
      annihilator equations.
\end{enumerate}

The calculation is presentation-relative because the choice of basis for
\(H_2\) and the invariant-factor coordinates of \(A\) are part of the input.
Changing either basis transports the matrices by invertible row or column
operations and leaves the abstract image subgroup unchanged through the
specified isomorphism.

\section{A narrow lower-language separation example}
\label{sec:auxiliary-separation}

This final section explains why the article accepts a presheaf lift as input
instead of claiming that arbitrary lift data arise naturally from a lower
pointwise semantics.  The example is intentionally narrow.  It establishes
only that one constant-free, one-reference-variable language cannot define a
matching-family predicate.  It does not produce a banded prestack and plays
no role in the proofs cited from the article.

Let \(L\) be a relational language with one distinguished sort \(R\), no
constants of sort \(R\), no parameters, and formulas having at most one free
variable \(x:R\).  Let \(\mathcal M\) be an \(L\)-structure and suppose a
finite family of elements of \(R^\mathcal M\) forms a single orbit under
\(\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal M)\).  Automorphism invariance gives

\begin{equation}\label{eq:orbit-invariance}
 \mathcal M\models\varphi(r)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \mathcal M\models\varphi(r')
\end{equation}

for every parameter-free one-variable formula \(\varphi\) and every two
elements \(r,r'\) in that orbit.

Attach to each \(r\in R^\mathcal M\) a finite pullback site
\(\mathcal C_r\) and a set-valued presheaf \(F_r\).  This attachment is
external to the language \(L\).  Choose two orbit elements \(r_0,r_1\) so
that:

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item \(F_{r_0}\) has local sections on a cover whose restrictions agree on
      every pullback overlap;
\item \(F_{r_1}\) has local sections on the corresponding cover, but at least
      one pair has unequal restrictions on a nonempty overlap.
\end{enumerate}

Such data can be realized on the cover with two members and one nonempty
overlap.  Give \(F_{r_0}\) a singleton on both cover members and on the
overlap, with the unique restriction maps.  Give \(F_{r_1}\) singleton
sets on the cover members, a two-element set on the overlap, and restriction
maps selecting different elements.  In both cases the terminal value may be
empty.

Equation \eqref{eq:orbit-invariance} shows that no formula in the stated
fragment distinguishes \(r_0\) from \(r_1\), while the attached presheaf
predicate ``there exists a strict matching family'' does distinguish them.
Therefore that predicate is not definable uniformly in this fragment from
the pointwise \(L\)-structure alone.

The restrictions on the language matter.  With two free reference
variables, equality may distinguish tuples by equality pattern.  With named
parameters, a formula \(x=r_0\) isolates the selected witness.  With symbols
that directly encode restriction maps or matching families, the predicate
may be definable by construction.  The example therefore supplies no broad
undefinability theorem.  It supports only the modest interface decision in
\href{run:main.pdf}{Section~\ref*{art-sec:preliminaries}}: the finite site,
presheaf, and banded prestack
are supplied data unless a separate source construction is proved.

The article's empirical-model boundary result,
\href{run:main.pdf}{Theorem~\ref*{art-thm:empirical-model-boundary}}, gives a
substantive instance of the
same caution.  A support presheaf canonically yields a split stack, but a
non-split banded lift and its degree-two class remain additional data.


 succeeded in 344ms:
\section{Finite good-cover sites and prescribed realization}
\label{sec:homological-visibility}

Let \(\mathcal U=\{U_i\}_{i\in I}\) be a finite open cover of a space
\(X\).  Its ordinary nerve \(N=N(\mathcal U)\) records which finite
intersections are nonempty.  In order to identify Cech cochains with one
copy of a coefficient group for each simplex of \(N\), we impose the
following hypothesis throughout this section:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}
 \begin{minipage}{0.82\textwidth}
 every nonempty finite intersection \(U_J=\bigcap_{j\in J}U_j\) is
 connected, and the chosen trivializations of the locally constant
 coefficient sheaf identify \(\Gamma(U_J,\mathscr A)\) coherently with
 the same group \(A\).
 \end{minipage}
\end{equation}
Without connectedness,
\(\Gamma(U_J,\mathscr A)\cong A^{\pi_0(U_J)}\), whereas the ordinary nerve
retains only the information that \(U_J\) is nonempty.  Thus
\eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis} is essential for the ordinary-nerve
formulation below.  A component nerve or local coefficients would be needed
without it.

For a fixed cover satisfying \eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}, define
the \emph{finite intersection site} \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) as follows.
Its objects are a terminal object \(a\), one object \(U_J\) for every
nonempty simplex \(J\) of \(N\), and a strict initial object \(\varnothing\)
representing all empty intersections.  There is an arrow
\(U_J\to U_K\) when \(K\subseteq J\), and
\[
 U_J\times_{U_L}U_K=
 \begin{cases}
 U_{J\cup K},&J\cup K\in N,\\
 \varnothing,&J\cup K\notin N.
 \end{cases}
\]
The pretopology is generated by the vertex cover \(\{U_i\to a\}\), its
pullbacks, identity covers, and the empty cover of \(\varnothing\).  Let
\(A_{\mathcal U}\) be the restriction of the sheaf of locally constant
\(A\)-valued functions on \(X\).  Hence
\[
 A_{\mathcal U}(U_J)=A,
 \qquad A_{\mathcal U}(\varnothing)=0,
\]
under the coherent trivializations in
\eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}.

\subsection*{Pullbacks, generated covers, and the initial object}

The formal initial object is part of the site, even though it contributes
zero to the nonempty Cech nerve.  For an arbitrary finite subset
\(J\subseteq I\), write
\[
 \widetilde U_J=
 \begin{cases}
 U_J,&J\in N,\\
 \varnothing,&J\notin N.
 \end{cases}
\]
If \(U_J\to U_L\) and \(U_K\to U_L\), then
\[
 U_J\times_{U_L}U_K=\widetilde U_{J\cup K}.
\]
Indeed, if \(J\cup K\) is a simplex, every intersection object mapping to
both factors is indexed by a simplex containing \(J\cup K\), and therefore
factors uniquely through \(U_{J\cup K}\).  If it is not a simplex, no
nonempty object maps to both factors and the universal object is
\(\varnothing\).  Pullbacks involving \(\varnothing\) are again
\(\varnothing\).  Thus disjoint cover members still have a fibre product in
the site.

Pulling the vertex cover back along \(U_J\to a\) gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:formal-pulled-back-cover}
 \{\widetilde U_{J\cup\{i\}}\longrightarrow U_J\}_{i\in I}.
\end{equation}
Terms equal to \(\varnothing\) may be repeated, but they are not silently
deleted from the pullback calculation.  They make no contribution to
matching for \(A_{\mathcal U}\) because the empty cover forces
\(A_{\mathcal U}(\varnothing)=0\).  The generated pretopology is obtained
by iterating \eqref{eq:formal-pulled-back-cover}, identity covers, and
transitivity.

There is a useful simplification away from the terminal object.  If
\(U_J\ne a,\varnothing\), then the pulled-back family contains the identity
of \(U_J\): choose any \(i\in J\), for which
\(\widetilde U_{J\cup\{i\}}=U_J\).  Every generated cover of a nonempty
object in the slice over \(U_J\) consequently refines a cover containing an
identity.  Descent for an abelian sheaf on this slice is therefore controlled
by its value at the terminal slice object.  Evaluation at that object is an
exact functor, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:formal-slice-acyclicity}
 H^q(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/U_J,A_{\mathcal U})=0
 \qquad(q>0).
\end{equation}
This supplies the Leray acyclicity internally for the formal good-cover
site.  If one begins with an actual topological good cover instead, the same
vanishing follows because every nonempty intersection is contractible.

\subsection*{Why connectedness is the exact ordinary-nerve hypothesis}

For a sheaf of locally constant \(A\)-valued functions,
\[
 \Gamma(U_J,A_X)\cong
 \prod_{C\in\pi_0(U_J)}A.
\]
An ordinary \(q\)-simplex \(J=\{i_0,\dots,i_q\}\) of \(N\) records only
that \(U_J\) is nonempty.  It does not record the set
\(\pi_0(U_J)\).  Hence an ordinary simplicial cochain assigns one element of
\(A\) to \(J\), while a Cech cochain assigns one element for every connected
component of \(U_J\).  The complexes coincide precisely under coherent
identifications
\[
 \Gamma(U_J,A_X)\cong A
\]
on every nonempty overlap.  Connectedness supplies the single copy, and the
chosen trivializations ensure that all face restrictions are the identity
of the same coefficient group.  This explains both parts of
\eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}; neither is only a notational
convenience.

Under these identifications, an ordered Cech cochain
\(c\in\check C^q(\mathcal U,A_{\mathcal U})\) is the family
\[
 c_{i_0\cdots i_q}\in A
 \qquad
 (i_0<\cdots<i_q,\ U_{i_0\cdots i_q}\ne\varnothing).
\]
Its differential is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cech-simplicial-differential}
 (\delta c)_{i_0\cdots i_{q+1}}
 =\sum_{r=0}^{q+1}(-1)^r
 c_{i_0\cdots\widehat{i_r}\cdots i_{q+1}},
\end{equation}
which is exactly the simplicial coboundary of \(N\).  If an overlap is
empty, its section group is zero and there is no corresponding simplex.
This gives the cochain-level, rather than merely cohomological,
identification used by the realization theorem.

\begin{proposition}[Connected-overlap and basis-site comparison]
\label{prop:finite-site-comparison}
Let \(A\) be an abelian group.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item For a finite cover satisfying
      \eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}, the ordered Cech complex of
      \(A_{\mathcal U}\) is naturally the simplicial cochain complex of the
      ordinary nerve:
      \[
      \check C^q(\mathcal U,A_{\mathcal U})
      \cong C^q(N,A).
      \]
\item If the nonempty intersections are also
      \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-acyclic, the Leray comparison gives
      \[
      H^q(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})
      \cong \check H^q(\mathcal U,A_{\mathcal U})
      \cong H^q(N,A).
      \]
\item Let \(K\) be a finite simplicial complex, put \(X=|K|\), and let
      \(\mathcal U^{(m)}\) be the open-star cover of
      \(\operatorname{Sd}^mK\).  The category \(\mathcal B_K\) of all open
      stars in these covers and all their finite intersections is a basis
      for \(X\), stable under finite intersections after adjoining the empty
      set.  The topology generated on \(\mathcal B_K\) is the induced
      open-cover topology, and restriction gives an equivalence
      \[
      \mathbf {Sh}(X)\simeq\mathbf {Sh}(\mathcal B_K).
      \]
      Under this equivalence the genuine constant sheaf on
      \(\mathcal B_K\) is the restriction of the ordinary constant sheaf on
      \(X\).  Every nonempty basic intersection is contractible, the covers
      \((\mathcal U^{(m)})_{m\ge0}\) are filtered and cofinal, and
      \[
      \varinjlim_m\check H^2(\mathcal U^{(m)},A)
      \cong H^2(X,A).
      \]
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For (i), an ordered Cech \(q\)-cochain assigns a section of
\(A_{\mathcal U}\) to each nonempty ordered \((q+1)\)-fold intersection.
Connectedness and the coherent trivializations identify that section group
with one copy of \(A\), and the restriction maps with the identity of
\(A\).  Empty intersections have section group zero.  The ordered Cech
coboundary is therefore exactly the alternating simplicial coboundary
\eqref{eq:cech-simplicial-differential}.  The identification commutes with
restriction to a subcover and with every refinement map compatible with the
chosen coefficient trivializations.

For (ii), the Cech-to-derived spectral sequence for \(\mathcal U\) has
\[
 E_1^{p,q}=\prod_{i_0<\cdots<i_p}
 H^q(U_{i_0\cdots i_p},A_{\mathcal U}).
\]
Acyclicity makes every row with \(q>0\) vanish, so the edge map is an
isomorphism.  Clause (i) identifies its remaining row with the nerve
cochain complex; this is the usual Leray theorem
\cite[Chap.~III, Sec.~4]{Bredon1997}.

For the formal intersection site, the same vanishing is visible directly in
\eqref{eq:formal-slice-acyclicity}.  The augmented Cech resolution
\[
 0\longrightarrow A_{\mathcal U}
 \longrightarrow
 \prod_i(j_i)_*A_{\mathcal U}|_{U_i}
 \longrightarrow
 \prod_{i<j}(j_{ij})_*A_{\mathcal U}|_{U_{ij}}
 \longrightarrow\cdots
\]
has acyclic terms because every nonempty intersection slice has exact global
sections.  Applying global sections computes derived cohomology by the same
ordered complex.  This makes clear that the comparison is a property of the
specified site and band, not of an abstract nerve alone.

For (iii), open stars in successive barycentric subdivisions have arbitrarily
small mesh.  Given \(x\in O\) with \(O\subseteq X\) open, a sufficiently
fine subdivision has a vertex whose open star contains \(x\) and lies in
\(O\).  Hence the union of all these stars is a basis.  Adding their finite
intersections makes it stable under intersections, and a family of basis
objects covers precisely when their union covers in \(X\).  Thus its
generated topology is the topology induced from the open-set site.

To make the small-mesh assertion explicit, fix a metric on the compact
polyhedron \(X\).  Barycentric subdivision reduces the mesh of each simplex
by a uniform factor strictly less than one.  Choose
\(\varepsilon>0\) with \(B(x,\varepsilon)\subseteq O\).  At a sufficiently
late subdivision, every simplex meeting \(x\) has diameter less than
\(\varepsilon/2\).  A vertex belonging to a smallest simplex whose closure
contains \(x\) then has open star contained in \(O\).  Applying this
argument to every member of a finite open cover and using compactness shows
that a sufficiently late star cover refines that cover.

The Comparison Lemma for a basis stable under intersections now gives the
displayed equivalence of sheaf categories; explicitly, restriction is fully
faithful because sections are recovered by gluing over basis covers, and its
quasi-inverse sends a basis sheaf to the sheaf whose sections on an open set
are compatible basis-local sections.  This also shows directly that locally
constant \(A\)-valued functions restrict to the genuine constant sheaf used
on \(\mathcal B_K\) \cite[Chap.~III, Sec.~7]{MacLaneMoerdijk1994}.

A nonempty intersection of open stars is the open star of the simplex spanned
by the corresponding vertices, hence is contractible.  Barycentric
subdivision refines the preceding cover, and every finite basis cover is
refined by a sufficiently late subdivision cover; the sequence is therefore
filtered and cofinal.  The basic intersections are acyclic for the constant
sheaf, so (ii), the sheaf-category equivalence, and cofinality give
\[
 \varinjlim_m\check H^2(\mathcal U^{(m)},A)
 \cong H^2(\mathcal B_K,A)
 \cong H^2(X,A).
\]
This proves every asserted comparison, including the passage from the
all-subdivision generated site to the usual topological site.

The order of the argument is essential.  Contractibility supplies
connectedness and acyclicity on each selected cover.  The basis comparison
identifies sheaves on the generated site with sheaves on \(X\).  Cofinality
then identifies the colimit over subdivision covers with the full Cech
colimit.  Only after these three checks is the final derived-cohomology
identification justified.
\end{proof}

We turn to the paper-specific packaging of the standard Cech-cocycle gerbe
construction.  The point is not that a class in \(H^2\) can be represented
by a gerbe; that is classical.  The content is simultaneous control of the
component presheaf, terminal essential surjectivity, neutral and non-neutral
labels, and the images of the evaluation homomorphisms.

\begin{theorem}[Finite-site realization of prescribed componentwise evaluation maps]
\label{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}
Let \(\mathcal U=\{U_i\}_{i\in I}\) be a finite good cover satisfying
\eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}, with connected nerve \(N\), and let
\(A\) be a finite abelian group.  Assume
\[
 H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Let \(\Lambda\) be a finite nonempty set and prescribe homomorphisms
\[
 \phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A
 \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda).
\]
Put \(\Lambda_0=\{\lambda:\phi_\lambda=0\}\).  Then there are a presheaf
\(F\) on \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) and an
\(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded prestack lift
\(\mathfrak P\), with stackification \(\mathfrak L\), such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item \(F(a)=\Lambda_0\), \((aF)(a)\cong\Lambda\), and
      \(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\cong\Lambda\);
\item the component gerbe \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\) has class
      \(\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)\) satisfying
      \[
      \operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda,
      \qquad
      \operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda})
      =\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda),
      \]
      and \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\) is neutral exactly when
      \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\);
\item \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially surjective, and
      its terminal objects occur exactly in the components indexed by
      \(\Lambda_0\);
\item \(F\) has a strict matching family on \(\mathcal U\), and
      \[
      \operatorname{MWA}(F,a)
      \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
      \Lambda_0=\varnothing.
      \]
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The universal coefficient sequence and \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) give a natural
isomorphism
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uct-realization-isomorphism}
 \eta_A:H^2(N,A)\xrightarrow{\sim}
 \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A).
\end{equation}
For every \(\lambda\), let \(\omega_\lambda\) be the inverse image of
\(\phi_\lambda\).  Choose a normalized simplicial cocycle
\(\alpha_\lambda\in Z^2(N,A)\) representing it, choosing
\(\alpha_\lambda=0\) when \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\).  Extend it alternately
to ordered triples and set it to zero on triples with a repeated index.

The choice \(\alpha_\lambda=0\) for a zero map is legitimate because
\(\eta_A\) is injective: \(\phi_\lambda=0\) implies
\(\omega_\lambda=0\).  Conversely, a nonzero prescribed map has a nonzero
class.  For pairwise distinct indices, alternating extension means
\[
 \alpha_{\lambda,\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)}
 =\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}.
\]
On every ordered \(3\)-simplex, the cocycle equation is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:realization-cocycle-equation}
 \alpha_{\lambda,jkl}-\alpha_{\lambda,ikl}
 +\alpha_{\lambda,ijl}-\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}=0.
\end{equation}
These are the only coefficient identities used in the groupoid
construction.

Define the component presheaf by
\[
 F(W)=
 \begin{cases}
 \Lambda,&W\ne a,\varnothing\text{ is a nonempty intersection object},\\
 \Lambda_0,&W=a,\\
 \{*\},&W=\varnothing.
 \end{cases}
\]
Restrictions among proper nonempty intersections are identities,
restrictions from \(a\) are the inclusion
\(\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda\), and restrictions to
\(\varnothing\) are unique.  A matching family on the vertex cover has a
single label on every connected component of the nerve.  Since \(N\) is
connected, its label is a unique element of \(\Lambda\).  Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:label-sheafification}
 (aF)(a)\cong\Lambda.
\end{equation}

Here is the sheafification calculation in detail.  A family
\((\lambda_i)_{i\in I}\) with \(\lambda_i\in F(U_i)=\Lambda\) is matching
exactly when
\[
 \lambda_i=\lambda_j
 \quad\text{for every edge }\{i,j\}\in N.
\]
No condition is imposed by an empty overlap because both restrictions land
in the singleton \(F(\varnothing)\).  Edgewise equality propagates along
paths in the connected \(1\)-skeleton of \(N\), so every matching family is
constant.  Conversely, each constant family is matching.  It determines a
section of \(aF\) over \(a\), and distinct constants remain distinct after
restriction because every nonempty cover member retains its label.

Every cover in the generated pretopology is obtained from the vertex cover
by pullback and transitivity.  On a nonempty proper intersection, a generated
cover contains an identity, so it neither creates nor identifies labels.
It follows that the vertex-cover calculation computes all terminal sections
of the sheafification.  On proper nonempty objects,
\((aF)(W)=\Lambda\), and on the initial object it is a singleton.

We next construct the prestack and verify its structure rather than citing a
separate realization result.  Fix \(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\).  Put
\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\varnothing\).  For a nonempty proper intersection
\(W\), let
\[
 I(W)=\{i\in I:W\subseteq U_i\}.
\]
The groupoid \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(W)\) has objects
\(x_i^{\lambda,W}\) for \(i\in I(W)\), and
\[
 \operatorname{Hom}(x_i^{\lambda,W},x_j^{\lambda,W})
 =A_{\mathcal U}(W)\cong A.
\]
Writing a morphism as \((b,i,j)\), define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:twisted-composition}
 (c,j,k)\circ(b,i,j)
 =(b+c+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},i,k).
\end{equation}
Normalization supplies identities and inverses.  Explicitly,
\[
 1_{x_i^{\lambda,W}}=(0,i,i),
 \qquad
 (b,i,j)^{-1}=(-b,j,i),
\]
because every cocycle coordinate with a repeated index is zero.  For three
composable arrows, the two parenthesizations are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 (d,k,l)\circ\bigl((c,j,k)\circ(b,i,j)\bigr)
 &=(b+c+d+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}
       +\alpha_{\lambda,ikl},i,l),\\
 \bigl((d,k,l)\circ(c,j,k)\bigr)\circ(b,i,j)
 &=(b+c+d+\alpha_{\lambda,jkl}
       +\alpha_{\lambda,ijl},i,l).
\end{aligned}
\]
They agree by \eqref{eq:realization-cocycle-equation}.  Associativity is
therefore exactly the cocycle identity
\[
 \alpha_{\lambda,ijk}+\alpha_{\lambda,ikl}
 =\alpha_{\lambda,jkl}+\alpha_{\lambda,ijl}.
\]
If \(V\subseteq W\), then \(I(W)\subseteq I(V)\); restriction keeps the
object index and applies the sheaf restriction to the coefficient.  These
functors compose strictly.  Over \(\varnothing\) take the terminal groupoid.

To verify the restriction assertion, write
\(\rho_{VW}:A_{\mathcal U}(W)\to A_{\mathcal U}(V)\).  The functor sends
\[
 x_i^{\lambda,W}\longmapsto x_i^{\lambda,V},
 \qquad
 (b,i,j)\longmapsto(\rho_{VW}(b),i,j).
\]
On nonempty connected intersections, the coherent trivializations identify
\(\rho_{VW}\) with the identity of \(A\).  Hence
\[
 \rho_{VW}(b+c+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk})
 =\rho_{VW}(b)+\rho_{VW}(c)+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},
\]
so restriction preserves \eqref{eq:twisted-composition}.  If
\(Z\subseteq V\subseteq W\), the equality
\(\rho_{ZV}\rho_{VW}=\rho_{ZW}\) gives literal equality of the restriction
functors.  This is strict functoriality, not functoriality only up to a
cartesian comparison arrow.

For fixed \(i,j\in I(W)\), the presheaf of arrows between the corresponding
objects on the slice over \(W\) is
\[
 V\longmapsto A_{\mathcal U}(V).
\]
It is the restriction of the genuine coefficient sheaf, and hence is a
sheaf.  This verifies morphism descent for every twisted summand.
Translation in the coefficient coordinate identifies
\[
 \underline{\operatorname{Aut}}(x_i^{\lambda,W})
 \cong A_{\mathcal U}|_{\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W}.
\]
Changing \(i\) transports this identification through an arrow
\((b,i,j)\); abelianness makes the induced conjugation on \(A\) trivial.
The identifications therefore constitute an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banding.

For \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), let \(\mathfrak P_\lambda\) be the neutral
one-object \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded stack on every nonempty object and the
terminal groupoid on \(\varnothing\).  This is a stack here, not merely a
one-object prestack: on the slice over a nonempty formal intersection, every
generated cover contains an identity, so the nonempty part of the topology
is trivial; the empty cover forces value zero at \(\varnothing\).  Evaluation
at the terminal slice object is exact, whence
\(H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W,A_{\mathcal U})=0\), and every object
descent torsor is trivial.

Concretely, descent data for the distinguished local object on a cover
\(\{V_r\to W\}\) are arrows represented by coefficients
\(d_{rs}\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_{rs})\) satisfying
\[
 d_{rs}+d_{st}=d_{rt}.
\]
They form an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-torsor cocycle.  The vanishing just noted
provides coefficients \(e_r\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_r)\) with
\(d_{rs}=e_s-e_r\).  Adjusting the local identifications by \(e_r\) makes
them agree and yields the global object.  Morphism descent is the ordinary
sheaf condition for \(A_{\mathcal U}\).  Thus the neutral summand is already
a stack on every relevant slice.

Set
\[
 \mathfrak P=\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathfrak P_\lambda
\]
fibrewise on nonempty objects, with a shared terminal fibre over
\(\varnothing\).  For two objects in a twisted fibre, the isomorphism
presheaf on the relevant slice is the restriction of the genuine sheaf
\(A_{\mathcal U}\); it is therefore a sheaf.  Automorphism sheaves are the
same sheaf, so \(\mathfrak P\) is an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded prestack.
Every nonempty proper fibre has connected components indexed by \(\Lambda\),
the terminal fibre has components \(\Lambda_0\), and the empty fibre has one
component.  Thus
\[
 \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)\cong F.
\]
Stackification and \eqref{eq:label-sheafification} prove (i).

There are two points in this coproduct verification.  First, over a nonempty
object there are no arrows between different \(\lambda\)-summands, so
isomorphism descent cannot change a label.  A matching object descent datum
therefore lies in one summand on each connected part of a cover, and the
connected nerve forces a single label.  Second, all summands restrict to the
same terminal groupoid over \(\varnothing\).  This causes no identification
on a nonempty overlap: the initial object has no arrow back to a nonempty
object and its unique descent datum contributes no coefficient.

For a twisted summand, every two named objects are isomorphic, since their
Hom set is the nonempty set \(A\).  Thus each such fibre is connected.
The neutral summand has one object and is connected as well.  It follows
objectwise that the component presheaf is exactly \(F\), including its
restriction \(\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda\) out of the terminal object.
The standard compatibility of stackification with components gives
\[
 \pi_0(\mathfrak L)\cong aF,
\]
and the terminal component sections are the labels in \(\Lambda\).

On \(U_i\), choose \(x_i^{\lambda,U_i}\); on \(U_{ij}\), choose the arrow
\((0,i,j)\).  By \eqref{eq:twisted-composition}, its triple-overlap defect is
\[
 (0,i,k)^{-1}(0,j,k)(0,i,j)
 =\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}
\]
under the band identification.  The Cech representative of the component
gerbe is therefore exactly \(\alpha_\lambda\).  The good-cover comparison
in \Cref{prop:finite-site-comparison} identifies its Giraud class with
\(\omega_\lambda\), and
\eqref{eq:uct-realization-isomorphism} gives
\(\operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda\).  By the standard
component-gerbe package, this gerbe is neutral exactly when its class is zero,
equivalently when \(\phi_\lambda=0\).  This proves (ii).

To check the sign in the defect, use
\[
 (0,i,k)^{-1}=(0,k,i)
\]
and apply \eqref{eq:twisted-composition} twice.  Normalization cancels the
terms with repeated indices, while alternating extension moves the remaining
term to the ordered coordinate \(\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}\).  This is the same
convention as \eqref{eq:giraud-triple-defect}.  Therefore the class obtained
by stackification is not merely cohomologous to a class with the desired
evaluation: its chosen vertex-cover representative is the initially
selected cocycle \(\alpha_\lambda\).

The connected-overlap hypothesis enters here a second time.  It identifies
the automorphism section on \(U_{ijk}\) with one element of \(A\), so the
defect is an ordinary simplicial coordinate.  If \(U_{ijk}\) were
disconnected, the same calculation would yield a tuple in
\(A^{\pi_0(U_{ijk})}\), and the prescribed ordinary-nerve cocycle would not
specify the entire defect.

For \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the neutral summand is already a stack and its
terminal object maps to the corresponding terminal object of
\(\mathfrak L\).  If \(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\), the component gerbe is
non-neutral and therefore has no object over \(a\).  Hence every object of
\(\mathfrak L(a)\) lies in a zero component and comes from
\(\mathfrak P(a)\), proving (iii), including the vacuous case
\(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\).

When \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\), an object of
\(\mathfrak L(a)\) has some component label
\(\lambda\in\Lambda\).  The component-gerbe package identifies the full
substack at that label with \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\).  If
\(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\), non-neutrality says that this full substack has
no terminal object, so the assumed object cannot have that label.  If
\(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the distinguished terminal object of the neutral
summand of \(\mathfrak P\) maps to an object in the same component of
\(\mathfrak L\).  Any other terminal object in that component is isomorphic
to it because the neutral component is a gerbe.  This is precisely essential
surjectivity, rather than equality of object sets.

Finally, assigning one fixed \(\lambda\in\Lambda\) to every \(U_i\) is a
strict matching family: restrictions on nonempty overlaps are identities and
those on empty overlaps are unique.  It has an amalgamation precisely when
\(F(a)=\Lambda_0\) is nonempty.  This proves (iv) and completes the
construction.

In terms of the definition from \Cref{sec:preliminaries}, the last
equivalence uses only existence.  If \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\), every
constant label gives a strict matching family and there is no global
section, so matching without amalgamation holds.  If
\(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\), \(F(a)\) is nonempty and the predicate
\(\operatorname{MWA}(F,a)\) is false, although matching families with
nonzero labels may still fail individually to amalgamate.  The theorem does
not claim that every matching family amalgamates in this case.
\end{proof}

The gerbes in this proof are obtained from the standard Cech-cocycle
construction.  What the theorem adds is the single finite-label prestack
whose sheaf of components, terminal fibre, neutrality pattern, and homological
images are all prescribed simultaneously.

\subsection*{The two terminal-fibre endpoints}

If every prescribed map is zero, then \(\Lambda_0=\Lambda\).  The component
presheaf is already constant on every nonempty object, all summands are
neutral, and the stackification has a terminal object in every global
component.  The theorem reduces to a finite coproduct of split
\(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded stacks.  Matching families and global presheaf
sections are both indexed by \(\Lambda\).

At the other endpoint, suppose every prescribed map is nonzero.  Then
\(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\), so
\[
 F(a)=\varnothing,\qquad
 (aF)(a)=\Lambda,\qquad
 \mathfrak P(a)=\varnothing.
\]
Every global component gerbe is non-neutral, hence
\(\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\) as well.  The map
\[
 \mathfrak P(a)\longrightarrow\mathfrak L(a)
\]
is essentially surjective because both fibres are empty.  This is the
vacuous terminal-essential-surjectivity case used by matching without
amalgamation.  It is not a claim that the non-neutral global component
sections have terminal objects; they do not.

For a mixed family, \(\mathfrak L(a)\) contains objects exactly in the
components indexed by \(\Lambda_0\).  The nonzero labels still occur as
sections of \(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\cong(aF)(a)\), and their full component
substacks are locally inhabited gerbes without terminal objects.  This is
the concrete distinction among the three terminal levels in
\eqref{eq:three-terminal-levels}.

\subsection*{A three-label calculation}

Take a triangulated \(2\)-sphere and an open-star cover of a barycentric
subdivision.  Its nerve \(N\) satisfies
\[
 H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0,\qquad H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong\mathbb Z.
\]
Let \(A=\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z\), and take the label set
\[
 \Lambda=\{\mathtt n,\mathtt p,\mathtt q\}.
\]
Prescribe
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \phi_{\mathtt n}(1)&=0,\\
 \phi_{\mathtt p}(1)&=2,\\
 \phi_{\mathtt q}(1)&=3.
\end{aligned}
\]
Then
\[
 \Lambda_0=\{\mathtt n\},\qquad
 K_{\mathtt n}=0,\qquad
 K_{\mathtt p}=\langle2\rangle,\qquad
 K_{\mathtt q}=\langle3\rangle.
\]
The three image groups have orders \(1,3,2\), respectively.  The UCT
isomorphism chooses three classes in \(H^2(N,A)\cong A\), and normalized
cocycles \(\alpha_{\mathtt n}=0\),
\(\alpha_{\mathtt p}\), and \(\alpha_{\mathtt q}\) feed the twisted
composition formula.

The component presheaf has
\[
 F(a)=\{\mathtt n\},
 \qquad
 F(U_J)=\{\mathtt n,\mathtt p,\mathtt q\}
\]
on every proper nonempty intersection.  Its sheafification has three
terminal component sections.  Only the \(\mathtt n\)-component has a
terminal stack object.  Therefore
\[
 \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
 =\{\mathtt n,\mathtt p,\mathtt q\},
 \qquad
 \pi_0(\mathfrak L(a))=\{\mathtt n\}.
\]
The notation on the right means the set of isomorphism classes of actual
terminal objects; it is smaller than the value at \(a\) of the component
sheaf.

For the two nonzero labels,
\[
 Q_{\mathtt p}=A/\langle2\rangle\cong\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z,
 \qquad
 Q_{\mathtt q}=A/\langle3\rangle\cong\mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z.
\]
Their images are complementary:
\[
 \langle2\rangle\cap\langle3\rangle=0,
 \qquad
 \langle2\rangle+\langle3\rangle=A.
\]
If all three labels are aggregated, the zero image from
\(\mathtt n\) keeps the intersection equal to zero, while the sum remains
\(A\).  Hence
\[
 Q_{\mathrm{lab}}=A,\qquad Q_{\mathrm{com}}=0.
\]

This mixed example does not satisfy matching without amalgamation because
\(F(a)\ne\varnothing\).  It does contain matching families with labels
\(\mathtt p\) and \(\mathtt q\) which individually have no amalgamation.
Deleting the neutral label produces the all-nonzero endpoint above: the same
two component classes remain, \(F(a)\) becomes empty, and the existence
predicate \(\operatorname{MWA}(F,a)\) becomes true.


 succeeded in 362ms:
\section{Homological images and aggregate quotients}
\label{sec:homological-images}

Fix a finite simplicial complex \(N\), a finite abelian group \(A\), and a
class \(\omega\in H^2(N,A)\).  The universal coefficient map is denoted by
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_\omega:
 H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A.
\]
Define its \emph{homological image} and \emph{homological cokernel} by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:homological-image-cokernel}
 K_\omega:=\operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{ev}_\omega),
 \qquad Q_\omega:=A/K_\omega.
\end{equation}
These definitions depend on the supplied finite presentation of the component
gerbe.  Refinement and retrivialization transport them by UCT naturality, but
no canonical identification along distinct comparison zigzags is asserted.
The resulting freely generated presentation groupoid and its elementary
naturality calculation are recorded in the technical appendix rather than
promoted to an invariance theorem.

For a finite family of component classes
\((\omega_v)_{v\in V}\), put \(K_v=K_{\omega_v}\) and define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:aggregate-quotients}
 Q_{\mathrm{lab}}:=A\Big/\bigcap_{v\in V}K_v,
 \qquad
 Q_{\mathrm{com}}:=A\Big/\sum_{v\in V}K_v.
\end{equation}
The first quotient retains enough information to map to every componentwise
quotient \(A/K_v\).  The second retains only characters which descend through
every one of them.

\begin{proposition}[UCT and aggregate subgroup package]
\label{prop:uct-aggregate-package}
With the notation above:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item The universal coefficient sequence is
      \[
      0\longrightarrow
      \operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)
      \longrightarrow H^2(N,A)
      \xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}}
      \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A)
      \longrightarrow0.
      \]
      Thus \(K_\omega=0\) exactly when \(\omega\) lies in the displayed
      Ext subgroup.  In particular, nonzero classes can have zero
      homological image when \(H_1\) has torsion.
\item The quotient map \(q_\omega:A\to Q_\omega\) is initial among group
      homomorphisms \(q:A\to B\) satisfying
      \(q\circ\operatorname{ev}_\omega=0\).  A character
      \(\chi:A\to\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\) factors through \(Q_\omega\)
      exactly when \(\chi(K_\omega)=0\).  Since finite Pontryagin duality
      separates points,
      \[
      K_\omega=\mathop{\bigcap}_{\chi(K_\omega)=0}\ker\chi.
      \]
\item For a nonempty finite family \((K_v)_{v\in V}\), there is a natural
      short exact sequence
      \begin{equation}\label{eq:aggregate-exact-sequence}
      0\longrightarrow
      \frac{\sum_vK_v}{\bigcap_vK_v}
      \longrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{lab}}
      \longrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{com}}
      \longrightarrow0.
      \end{equation}
      Moreover,
      \[
      \widehat{Q_{\mathrm{com}}}
      \cong\bigcap_{v\in V}K_v^\perp,
      \qquad
      \widehat{Q_{\mathrm{lab}}}
      \cong\sum_{v\in V}K_v^\perp
      \]
      inside \(\widehat A\).
\item If \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) and
      \(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong\mathbb Z^\beta\), a subgroup
      \(K\le A\) occurs as \(K_\omega\) exactly when \(d(K)\le\beta\).
      Every finite family of such subgroups is simultaneously attained by
      the prestack of
      \Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Clause (i) is the cohomological universal coefficient theorem, natural in
both \(N\) and \(A\) \cite[Sec.~3.1]{Hatcher2002}.  Its middle-to-right map
evaluates a cohomology class on integral \(2\)-cycles, so its value at
\(\omega\) is \(\operatorname{ev}_\omega\).  Exactness identifies the
kernel with the Ext term.

For a simplicial map \(f:N'\to N\), naturality is the commutative square
\[
\begin{CD}
H^2(N,A) @>{\operatorname{ev}}>>
 \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A)\\
@V{f^*}VV @VV{(-)\circ f_*}V\\
H^2(N',A) @>{\operatorname{ev}}>>
 \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N',\mathbb Z),A).
\end{CD}
\]
Thus
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_{f^*\omega}
 =\operatorname{ev}_\omega\circ f_*.
\]
For a coefficient homomorphism \(\theta:A\to A'\), the corresponding
identity is
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_{\theta_*\omega}
 =\theta\circ\operatorname{ev}_\omega.
\]
These formulas are the only invariance input used for a specified
presentation comparison.  The short exact UCT sequence may split as a
sequence of groups, but no natural splitting is chosen.

For (ii), \(q\circ\operatorname{ev}_\omega=0\) says precisely that \(q\)
vanishes on \(K_\omega\).  The universal property of the quotient gives a
unique factorization through \(A/K_\omega\).  Apply the same observation to
characters.  The intersection formula follows because characters of a
finite abelian group separate points \cite{Terras1999}.

For the last assertion, pass to the finite quotient \(A/K_\omega\).  If
\(a\notin K_\omega\), then \(\bar a\ne0\) in that quotient.  Finite
Pontryagin duality gives a character
\[
 \bar\chi:A/K_\omega\longrightarrow\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z
\]
with \(\bar\chi(\bar a)\ne0\).  Its pullback \(\chi=\bar\chi q_\omega\)
annihilates \(K_\omega\) but not \(a\).  Hence no element outside
\(K_\omega\) belongs to the displayed intersection of kernels.  The reverse
containment is immediate.

The containment \(\bigcap_vK_v\subseteq\sum_vK_v\) gives the quotient map in
\eqref{eq:aggregate-exact-sequence}.  By the third isomorphism theorem its
kernel is
\[
 \frac{\sum_vK_v}{\bigcap_vK_v},
\]
which proves exactness.  For finite abelian groups, annihilators exchange sums and
intersections:
\[
 \Big(\sum_vK_v\Big)^\perp=\bigcap_vK_v^\perp,
 \qquad
 \Big(\bigcap_vK_v\Big)^\perp=\sum_vK_v^\perp.
\]
Dualizing the two quotients proves the character formulas in (iii).

The first annihilator identity does not require finiteness:
\(\chi\) kills \(\sum_vK_v\) exactly when it kills every \(K_v\).  For the
second, one inclusion is immediate:
\[
 \sum_vK_v^\perp\subseteq\Big(\bigcap_vK_v\Big)^\perp.
\]
For finite groups, equality follows from exactness of Pontryagin duality.
It may also be checked by cardinalities, using
\[
 |H^\perp|=\frac{|A|}{|H|}
\]
and, for two subgroups,
\[
 |K+L|\,|K\cap L|=|K|\,|L|.
\]
Induction gives the formula for a finite family.

Under the hypotheses of (iv), the Ext term vanishes and UCT becomes
\[
 H^2(N,A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z^\beta,A).
\]
The image of a homomorphism from \(\mathbb Z^\beta\) is generated by the
images of the standard basis, so it has at most \(\beta\) generators.
Conversely, given generators \(k_1,\dots,k_\beta\) of \(K\), allowing zeros
when fewer are needed, the map
\[
 (n_1,\dots,n_\beta)\longmapsto\sum_jn_jk_j
\]
has image \(K\).  Applying
\Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction} to a family of these maps
gives simultaneous realization.

The number \(d(K)\) can be read prime by prime.  If
\[
 K\cong\bigoplus_p\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r_p}C_{p^{e_{p,j}}},
\]
then
\[
 d(K)=\max_p r_p.
\]
Thus the condition in (iv) is not a bound on \(|K|\) or on its exponent.
It is precisely the rank bound imposed by the \(\beta\) basis elements of
\(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\).  Once homomorphisms with the desired images have been
chosen componentwise, simultaneous attainment is the prestack-level part
supplied by \Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{How to read the aggregate quotients}

There are canonical maps
\[
 Q_{\mathrm{lab}}\longrightarrow A/K_v
 \qquad(v\in V),
\]
because \(\bigcap_wK_w\subseteq K_v\).  The product of these maps is
injective:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:labelled-product-embedding}
 A/\bigcap_vK_v
 \longrightarrow\prod_{v\in V}A/K_v,
 \qquad
 a+\bigcap_vK_v\longmapsto(a+K_v)_v.
\end{equation}
Its kernel consists of the elements lying in every \(K_v\).  Thus
\(Q_{\mathrm{lab}}\) is the part of the coefficient group retained by all
componentwise quotient values together.

In the other direction, every componentwise quotient maps onto
\(Q_{\mathrm{com}}\):
\[
 A/K_v\longrightarrow A/\sum_wK_w,
\]
because \(K_v\subseteq\sum_wK_w\).  This is the largest quotient of \(A\)
of the displayed form receiving the quotient map from every \(A/K_v\).
The map in \eqref{eq:aggregate-exact-sequence} measures the loss between
keeping the tuple of component values and keeping only their common quotient.

On characters, the directions reverse.  A character of
\(Q_{\mathrm{com}}\) must kill every \(K_v\), while a character of
\(Q_{\mathrm{lab}}\) is a finite sum of characters each killing one of the
component subgroups.  Written inside \(\widehat A\), these are precisely the
two annihilator identities in the proposition.  This interpretation uses
only the contravariance of finite duality.

\subsection*{Evaluation at chain level}

Choose a simplicial cocycle
\(\alpha\in Z^2(N,A)\) representing \(\omega\).  If
\[
 z=\sum_{\sigma\in N_2}n_\sigma\sigma
\]
is an integral \(2\)-cycle, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:chain-level-evaluation}
 \operatorname{ev}_\omega([z])
 =\langle\alpha,z\rangle
 =\sum_{\sigma\in N_2}n_\sigma\alpha(\sigma).
\end{equation}
Replacing \(\alpha\) by \(\alpha+\delta b\) changes the last expression by
\[
 \langle\delta b,z\rangle
 =\langle b,\partial z\rangle=0.
\]
Replacing \(z\) by \(z+\partial c\) changes it by
\[
 \langle\alpha,\partial c\rangle
 =\langle\delta\alpha,c\rangle=0.
\]
Thus \eqref{eq:chain-level-evaluation} depends only on the cohomology class
and the homology class.  In the realization theorem,
\(\alpha=\alpha_\lambda\) is also the triple-overlap defect of the
\(\lambda\)-component gerbe, so the formula evaluates that defect directly
on a \(2\)-cycle of the chosen nerve.

This chain formula also explains why the coordinate subgroup
\[
 H_\alpha=\langle\alpha(\sigma):\sigma\in N_2\rangle
\]
can be larger than \(K_\omega\).  Every value in \(K_\omega\) is an integral
combination of cocycle coordinates, so \(K_\omega\subseteq H_\alpha\).
But the coefficients in \eqref{eq:chain-level-evaluation} must satisfy the
cycle equations.  An arbitrary combination of coordinates need not arise
from a cycle.  Moreover, \(H_\alpha\) can change when \(\alpha\) is altered
by a coboundary, whereas \(K_\omega\) cannot.

\subsection*{Torsion and free-homology boundary cases}

Suppose
\[
 H_1(N,\mathbb Z)
 \cong\mathbb Z^r\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^t\mathbb Z/n_j\mathbb Z.
\]
The free summand contributes nothing to the Ext term, while
\[
 \operatorname{Ext}^1(\mathbb Z/n_j\mathbb Z,A)
 \cong A/n_jA.
\]
Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:explicit-ext-term}
 \operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)
 \cong\bigoplus_{j=1}^t A/n_jA.
\end{equation}
If every multiplication map \(n_j:A\to A\) is surjective, the Ext term
vanishes even though \(H_1\) has torsion.  For finite \(A\), shared primary
torsion between \(H_1\) and \(A\) is what permits classes that evaluation on
\(H_2\) cannot detect.

For example, if
\[
 H_1(N,\mathbb Z)\cong\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z,
 \qquad H_2(N,\mathbb Z)=0,
 \qquad A=\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z,
\]
then \(H^2(N,A)\cong\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z\) entirely through
\eqref{eq:explicit-ext-term}.  The nonzero class defines a non-neutral
banded gerbe, but
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_\omega:0\longrightarrow A
\]
has zero image and \(Q_\omega=A\).  The quotient does not detect neutrality
in this case.

At the opposite endpoint, if \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)\) is free, the Ext term
vanishes.  Then
\[
 H^2(N,A)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A)
\]
is an isomorphism.  A class has zero homological image only when it is zero.
The wedge-of-spheres application lies in this regime.

\subsection*{A two-component arithmetic check}

Let
\[
 A=\mathbb Z/12\mathbb Z,\qquad
 K_1=\langle4\rangle,\qquad
 K_2=\langle3\rangle.
\]
Then \(K_1\) has order \(3\), \(K_2\) has order \(4\), and
\[
 K_1\cap K_2=0,\qquad K_1+K_2=A.
\]
Hence
\[
 Q_{\mathrm{lab}}=A,\qquad Q_{\mathrm{com}}=0,
\]
and the kernel in \eqref{eq:aggregate-exact-sequence} is \(A\).  Both
subgroups are cyclic, so each is the image of a homomorphism
\(\mathbb Z\to A\).  On a nerve with
\(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong\mathbb Z\) and \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\), the
prescribed-realization theorem places these maps in two component gerbes of
a single prestack.

By contrast, if \(A=\mathbb Z/8\mathbb Z\), any two nonzero subgroups are
comparable:
\[
 \langle4\rangle\subseteq\langle2\rangle\subseteq A.
\]
Their intersection cannot be zero.  Thus no two nonzero component images
can satisfy \(K_1\cap K_2=0\) and \(K_1+K_2=A\).  This elementary contrast
is the cyclic part of the arithmetic used in the next section.

All clauses except simultaneous prestack realization are standard formal
consequences of UCT, finite duality, and subgroup arithmetic.  In particular,
there is no separate information-theoretic theorem hidden in the fibres of
these quotients.  Since a quotient fibre is a coset of its kernel, the minimum
cardinality of an unrestricted auxiliary set capable of separating every
fibre is simply the kernel cardinality.  For example,
\[
 |q_\omega^{-1}(\bar a)|=|K_\omega|,
 \qquad
 \left|\ker(Q_{\mathrm{lab}}\to Q_{\mathrm{com}})\right|
 =\left|\frac{\sum_vK_v}{\bigcap_vK_v}\right|.
\]

The Ext clause also marks a limitation of the homological image.  If
\(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) and
\(\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)\ne0\), a nonzero gerbe class can
have \(K_\omega=0\) and \(Q_\omega=A\).  For instance, an
\(\mathbb RP^2\)-type nerve and suitable \(2\)-torsion coefficients exhibit
this behavior.  Conversely, when \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)\) is free, the Ext term
vanishes and zero evaluation forces \(\omega=0\).  These are UCT boundary
cases, not additional classification results.


 succeeded in 361ms:
